{"forms":[{"form":"Misses","tags":["plural"]},{"form":"Mlles","tags":["plural"]}],"lang_code":"en","pos":"noun","senses":[{"glosses":["A form of address, now used chiefly for an unmarried woman; used chiefly of girls before the mid-1700s, and thereafter used also of adult women without regard to marital status."]},{"examples":[{"text":"These are the two young ladies that I wanted you to meet, Miss Jones and Miss Robinson.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["A form of address, now used chiefly for an unmarried woman; used chiefly of girls before the mid-1700s, and thereafter used also of adult women without regard to marital status.","With a surname."]},{"examples":[{"text":"The victim was named as Miss Jane Doe.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["A form of address, now used chiefly for an unmarried woman; used chiefly of girls before the mid-1700s, and thereafter used also of adult women without regard to marital status.","With a full name."]},{"examples":[{"text":"Excuse me Ma'am, will Miss Julia and Miss Emily be staying for tea?","type":"example"}],"glosses":["A form of address, now used chiefly for an unmarried woman; used chiefly of girls before the mid-1700s, and thereafter used also of adult women without regard to marital status.","With a first name only."],"tags":["dated","regional"]},{"examples":[{"text":"Good morning, Miss.
